class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int

        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = ptr2 = 0

        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

        s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(s2))
        
        while True:
            for char in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(char, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(char)
                start = ptr+1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] >= n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

            if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(rec)-1
            else: break
        
        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart
        
        rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

        while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2

Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int

        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = ptr2 = 0

        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

        s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(s2))
        
        while True:
            for char in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(char, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(char)
                start = ptr+1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] >= n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

            if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(rec)-1
            else: break
        
        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart
        
        rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

        while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int

        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = ptr2 = 0

        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

        s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(s2))
        
        while True:
            for char in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(char, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(char)
                start = ptr+1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] >= n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

            if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(rec)-1
            else: break
        
        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart
        
        rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

        while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2
```

def getMaxRepetitions(s1, n1, s2, n2):
    if not set(s2) <= set(s1): return 0
    repeat = [0]  # repeat[i] = number of s1 consumed to match i copies of s2?

    index = 0

        for ch in s2:
            index = s1.find(ch, index)
            if index == -1:
                cnt += 1
                index = s1.find(ch)
            index += 1
        repeat.append(cnt+1)
        # ...

Pseudo:

```
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in set(s2))

        rec = [0]  # rec[k] = number of s1 repetitions needed to match k copies of s2
        track = {}
        cnt = 0
        start = 0

        k = 0
        while True:
            # match one copy of s2
            for ch in s2:
                pos = s1.find(ch, start)
                if pos == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    pos = s1.find(ch)
                start = pos + 1
            k += 1
            rec.append(cnt + 1)

            if rec[k] > n1:
                return (k - 1) // n2

            ptr = start - 1  # position of last matched char in s1
            if ptr in track:
                h = track[ptr]
                # cycle from h to k
                cycle_copies = k - h
                cycle_reps = rec[k] - rec[h]
                rest = n1 - rec[h]
                full_cycles = rest // cycle_reps
                rem_reps = rest % cycle_reps

                # Find extra copies within rem_reps after full cycles.
                # We can use rec[h..k] because rem_reps < cycle_reps.
                # Need max t in [0, cycle_copies] such that rec[h+t] - rec[h] <= rem_reps.
                # Since rec is nondecreasing, scan or binary search.
                extra = 0
                while extra < cycle_copies and rec[h + extra + 1] - rec[h] <= rem_reps:
                    extra += 1

                total_copies = h + full_cycles * cycle_copies + extra
                return total_copies // n2
            else:
                track[ptr] = k
```

However, is `ptr = start - 1` always correct? If start can be len(s1)+? No, after find pos in [0,len(s1)-1], start=pos+1 in [1,len(s1)]. ptr=start-1 in [0,len(s1)-1]. Good. But if `s1.find(ch)` after wrap returns pos, start=pos+1. If char not found? But s1 filtered contains all chars in s2, so find should not return -1. But if s1 empty? If s2 subset of s1 and s1 filtered nonempty. Good.

But there is a subtlety: If we filter s1, the number of repetitions consumed rec[k] counts repetitions of filtered s1. Original s1 repetitions have same count. Good.

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        repeatCount = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        nextIndex = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        j = 0
        count = 0
        for k in range(1, n1 + 1):
            for i in range(len(s2)):
                if s1[j] == s2[i]:
                    i += 1
                if i == len(s2):
                    count += 1
                    i = 0
            ...

Let's write final code:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        allowed = set(s2)
        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]  # rec[k] = number of s1 repetitions needed for k copies of s2
        track = {}
        cnt = 0
        start = 0
        k = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                pos = s1.find(ch, start)
                if pos == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    pos = s1.find(ch)
                start = pos + 1

            k += 1
            rec.append(cnt + 1)

            if rec[k] > n1:
                return (k - 1) // n2

            ptr = start - 1
            if ptr in track:
                h = track[ptr]
                cycle_copies = k - h
                cycle_reps = rec[k] - rec[h]
                rest = n1 - rec[h]

                full_cycles = rest // cycle_reps
                rem_reps = rest % cycle_reps

                extra = 0
                while extra < cycle_copies and rec[h + extra + 1] - rec[h] <= rem_reps:
                    extra += 1

                total_copies = h + full_cycles * cycle_copies + extra
                return total_copies // n2
            else:
                track[ptr] = k
```

Need check if `ptr` could be -1? After matching a copy, start at least 1, so ptr >=0. If s2 empty? not.

But wait: If `s1.find(ch)` after wrap returns -1? We filtered s1 to chars in s2, so every char in s2 appears in s1. But what if s1 filtered is empty? no. Good.

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        repeat = [0]
        nextIdx = [0]
        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        for _ in range(n1):
            for ch in s2:
                idx = s1.find(ch, idx)
                if idx == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    idx = s1.find(ch)
                idx += 1
            repeat.append(cnt+1)
            nextIdx.append(idx)
            for j in range(len(repeat)-1):
                if nextIdx[j] == idx:
                    # cycle
```

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in set(s2))
        repeat = [0]
        next_idx = [0]
        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        for _ in range(n1):
            for ch in s2:
                idx = s1.find(ch, idx)
                if idx == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    idx = s1.find(ch)
                idx += 1
            repeat.append(cnt+1)
            next_idx.append(idx)
            for j in range(len(repeat)-1):
                if next_idx[j] == idx:
                    prev = repeat[j]
                    cycles = (repeat[-1] - prev)
                    ...
```

Before final, let's further scrutinize the algorithm with a formal proof and edge cases.

Formal model:
- Let F(s, start) be greedy matching of one copy of s2 in infinite repetition of s1 starting at index `start` (0 <= start <= len(s1)), returning (new_start, wraps) where wraps is number of times find failed from current position and wrapped. Actually if start == len(s1), first find fails and wraps. `cnt` accumulates wraps.
- `rec[k] = 1 + total_wraps_after_k_copies`.
- State `start_k` = start after k copies. `ptr_k = start_k - 1` if k>0.
- If `start_k` repeats, then future sequence of `(wraps per copy)` repeats with period.

Our cycle detection:
- We detect `ptr_k == ptr_h`, so `start_k == start_h`.
- For any t>=0, matching t copies from state h and from state k yields same sequence of starts and same number of wraps per copy. Thus `rec[h+t] - rec[h] = rec[k+t] - rec[k]` for t up to? Actually because state same, the number of wraps for the next copy is same, so rec increments same. Thus for t=0..cycle_copies, rec[h+t] = rec[h] + (rec[k]-rec[h])? Wait if h and k are occurrences, the sequence from h to k is one period. For t beyond k, rec repeats with period c and rec increment d. So yes.

Given n1, we can match copies as long as rec <= n1? Actually if rec == n1, still can match more copies with no additional wraps. But rec is nondecreasing. The condition for a copy to be matchable is that during matching it, the number of wraps after copy <= n1? If a copy requires wraps such that rec after copy > n1, then it enters repetition n1+1, so not matchable. If rec after copy == n1, it enters nth repetition, matchable. So max copies = max k with rec[k] <= n1? Wait if rec[k] == n1, matchable. If rec[k] > n1, not. Since rec nondecreasing, max k is last index with rec <= n1. But our early return when rec[k] > n1 returns k-1. If rec[k] == n1, we continue because there may be later indices with rec == n1. So ultimately max k = last index with rec <= n1. In cycle formula, we compute max k with rec[k] <= n1. Good.

Now, in cycle formula, `rest = n1 - rec[h]`. We want max t such that rec[h+t] <= n1. For t = q*c + r, rec[h+t] = rec[h] + q*d + (rec[h+r]-rec[h])? Actually because rec over periods: rec[h + q*c + r] = rec[h] + q*d + (rec[h+r]-rec[h]) for 0<=r<c, assuming rec[h+r] is within first period. We need <= n1. Since rec[h+r]-rec[h] < d for r<c? Is it strictly < d? rec[h+c]-rec[h] = d, and for r<c, rec[h+r] <= rec[h+c]? Could equal d if rec has duplicates? Wait rec[h+c] = rec[h]+d. For r<c, rec[h+r] could equal rec[h]+d? That would mean the period's total wraps occurred before the last copy, and the last copies had 0 wraps. Is that possible? Yes, if within cycle, all wraps happen early, then remaining copies consume no additional wraps. Example s1="aa", s2="a", cycle h=1,k=3,c=2,d=1: rec[1]=1, rec[2]=1, rec[3]=2. For r=1 (<c=2), rec[h+r]=rec[2]=1, rec[h]+d=2, not equal. Could rec[h+r] = rec[h]+d for r<c? That would mean rec[h+c] = rec[h+r] (no increase after r), so d wraps occurred by r, and remaining c-r copies had 0 wraps. Is that possible while returning to same ptr? If remaining copies have 0 wraps, start advances within same repetition; to return to same start after c copies, the total wraps d must account for full cycles. If all wraps occurred by r, then after r copies start maybe same? Let's construct: s1="abc", s2="abc"? d=1,c=1. Not. Need c>1. Suppose s1="ab", s2="ab"? c=1. Need c=2,d=1: two copies consume one wrap total and return to same ptr. Example s1="aab", s2="ab"? We found c=1,d=1. Let's find c=2,d=1. Need two copies of s2 total length L, start same, one wrap. Total advance = len(s1)+0 = len(s1). So 2*len(s2) = len(s1). Example s1 length4, s2 length2. s1="abab", s2="ab". Simulate:

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in set(s2))
        dp = [0]
        pos = [0]
        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        while True:
            for c in s2:
                idx = s1.find(c, idx)
                if idx == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    idx = s1.find(c)
                idx += 1
            dp.append(cnt+1)
            pos.append(idx)
            for j in range(len(dp)-1):
                if pos[j] == idx:
                    ...
```

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        allowed = set(s2)
        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        cnt = 0
        start = 0
        k = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                pos = s1.find(ch, start)
                if pos == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    pos = s1.find(ch)
                start = pos + 1

            k += 1
            rec.append(cnt + 1)

            if rec[k] > n1:
                return (k - 1) // n2

            ptr = start - 1
            if ptr in track:
                h = track[ptr]
                cycle_copies = k - h
                cycle_reps = rec[k] - rec[h]
                rest = n1 - rec[h]

                full_cycles = rest // cycle_reps
                rem_reps = rest % cycle_reps

                extra = 0
                while extra < cycle_copies and rec[h + extra + 1] - rec[h] <= rem_reps:
                    extra += 1

                return (h + full_cycles * cycle_copies + extra) // n2

            track[ptr] = k
```

s1="aa", s2="a", n1=2:

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        allowed = set(s2)
        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        cnt = 0
        start = 0
        k = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                pos = s1.find(ch, start)
                if pos == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    pos = s1.find(ch)
                start = pos + 1

            k += 1
            rec.append(cnt + 1)

            if rec[k] > n1:
                return (k - 1) // n2

            ptr = start - 1
            if ptr in track:
                h = track[ptr]
                cycle_copies = k - h
                cycle_reps = rec[k] - rec[h]
                rest = n1 - rec[h]

                full_cycles = rest // cycle_reps
                rem_reps = rest % cycle_reps

                extra = 0
                while extra < cycle_copies and rec[h + extra + 1] - rec[h] <= rem_reps:
                    extra += 1

                return (h + full_cycles * cycle_copies + extra) // n2

            track[ptr] = k
```

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        allowed = set(s2)
        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in allowed)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        cnt = 0
        start = 0
        k = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                pos = s1.find(ch, start)
                if pos == -1:
                    cnt += 1
                    pos = s1.find(ch)
                start = pos + 1

            k += 1
            rec.append(cnt + 1)

            if rec[k] > n1:
                return (k - 1) // n2

            ptr = start - 1
            if ptr in track:
                h = track[ptr]
                cycle_copies = k - h
                cycle_reps = rec[k] - rec[h]
                rest = n1 - rec[h]

                full_cycles = rest // cycle_reps
                rem_reps = rest % cycle_reps

                extra = 0
                while extra < cycle_copies and rec[h + extra + 1] - rec[h] <= rem_reps:
                    extra += 1

                return (h + full_cycles * cycle_copies + extra) // n2

            track[ptr] = k
